### Q15. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> contains the line of intersection of the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo></math>then the square of distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>)</mo></math> from the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>620</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>155</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>310</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1240</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>620</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given,</p><p>A plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> contains the line of intersection of the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></math>,</p><p>Now let, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, by family of planes concept we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Also given plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>Hence, equation of plane will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from plane will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mn>12</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mfenced><mn>12</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mfenced><mn>18</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>25</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>49</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>81</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>And square of distance will be,</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mn>12</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mfenced><mn>12</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mfenced><mn>18</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>25</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>49</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>81</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>310</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>310</mn></mrow><mn>155</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>620</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>13</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>14</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given,</p><p>The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math></p><p>Now let, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now using family of planes we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now let,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is parallel to line,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So, using perpendicular condition as normal vector of plane will be perpendicular to given line we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>22</mn><mi>x</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>14</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mi>z</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now on comparing with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>11</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> be the equation of a plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to the line joining the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi></math> is equal to _____ .

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> be the equation of a plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to the line joining the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>,</p><p>Now, normal vector of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, the equation of Plane will be,  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now given, point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> satisfies the plane</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Hence, the equation of plane will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Now comparing with given equation of plane, we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p>

### Q18. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> pass through the intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>, and be perpendicular to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math> is the distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>250</mn><mn>83</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>53</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>83</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>250</mn><mn>82</mn></mfrac></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>250</mn><mn>83</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> pass through the intersection of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> can be written as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8869;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>46</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>46</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Since, the distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math> from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>)</mo></math> to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>A</mi><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>B</mi><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>D</mi></mrow></mfenced><msqrt><msup><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Now the distance of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> from point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>42</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>46</mn></mrow></mfenced><msqrt><mn>36</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>169</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>625</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>50</mn><msqrt><mn>830</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn></mrow><mn>830</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>250</mn><mn>83</mn></mfrac></math></p>

### Q19. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let the image of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> in the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math>. Then the distance of the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> is

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>24</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>7</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></math>

<p>We have,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/00d896f1-d777-4e44-b41b-2f888ba1a9a6-9896645.png" style="width: 228px; height: 260px;" /></p><p>Equation of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>M</mi></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p>Any point on the line is</p><p><math <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math> lies on the plane, so</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Distance of the point <math open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>29</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math ### Q20. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The foot of perpendicular from the origin <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> to a plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> which meets the co-ordinate axes at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>N</mi></math>. If the volume of the tetrahedron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>144</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>unit</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math>, then which of the following points is NOT on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Plotting the diagram of the given data we get,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1f6d1cb7-8113-4dc9-8a51-02b3befa4899-9896469.png" style="width: 300px; height: 230px;" /></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math> is the foot of perpendicular from origin,</p><p>So, direction ratio of normal vector will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8801;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>So, equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Now Volume of tetrahedron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math> is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>O</mi><mi>B</mi><mo>&#183;</mo><mi>O</mi><mi>C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>144</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>a</mi></math></p><p>Let  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>k</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><math mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>k</mi><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mi>k</mi><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>k</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So, equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p>Clearly all three points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math> satisfy the equation of plane but <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math> does not satisfy plane</p>

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be the plane, passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to the line joining the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then the distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be the plane, passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to the line joining the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>,</p><p>So, direction ratio of Plane Normal will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now, equation of Plane will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Hence, perpendicular distance of plane from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> will be,</p><p>Distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>35</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>35</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> be parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>. If the intercept of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> on the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> , then the distance between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>6</mn><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p>The plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> be parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math> and the intercept of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> on the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5f48fbac-6034-43c0-9084-6a2a53d555d3-9896384.png" style="width: 350px; height: 194px;" /></p><p> </p><p>Now normal of the plane will be perpendicular to the given line, so by perpendicular condition we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> intercept of plane put <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> in equation of plane</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p>Put <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> in equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math> we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#945;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>Hence, equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If a plane passes through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and is parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>17</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>17</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>13</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>13</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p>A plane passes through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and is parallel to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>,</p><p>So, on rearranging <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Now given point : <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> </p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></math>, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></math></p><p>And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>C</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo mathvariant="italic">&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>)</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>The line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> is perpendicular to normal vector.</p><p>Now using the condition of perpendicular vector we get, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>26</mn><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>13</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A vector <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> in the first octant is inclined to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> axis at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>, to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> and to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>-axis at an acute angle. If a plane passing through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, is normal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Direction cosines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>n</mi></math> of given vector <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><msup><mn>60</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msup></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><msup><mn>45</mn><mo>&#8728;</mo></msup></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>cos</mi><mfenced><mi>&#947;</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>v</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>cos</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>cos</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#947;</mi><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>We know,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>l</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>m</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></math>Acute<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math></p><p>Equation of plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> lies on it,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

If  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are two values of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi></math> such that the angle between the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi mathvariant="italic">k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>sin</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math>, then the square of the length of perpendicular from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>38</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>10</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **315**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math></p><p>So, normal vector is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>9</mn></math></p><p>So, normal vector is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Now, angle between planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>sin</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Also, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mfenced open="|" close="|"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mfenced open="|" close="|"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt><mo>&#183;</mo><msqrt><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>Squaring both sides, we get</p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>25</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>9</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>64</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>48</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>35</mn><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>45</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>320</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>240</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>38</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>250</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>310</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>19</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>120</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>19</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>95</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>125</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>19</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Since, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>, so <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>19</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Perpendicular distance of point</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>38</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>10</mn><msub><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> close="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>105</mn><msqrt><mn>35</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Square of the distance is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>105</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>105</mn></mrow><mn>35</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>315</mn></math></p>

### Q26. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

If the equation of the plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and perpendicular to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>140</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is equal to

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **15**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of the required plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>Since, required plane is perpendicular to the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi></math>, therefore normal vector of the plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo mathvariant="italic">^</mo></mover></math></p><p>So, direction ratios of normal to the plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>11</mn></math></p><p>Equation of plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>28</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>28</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi></mrow><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>28</mn><mn>11</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Comparing with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>C</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>140</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>A</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>140</mn><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>11</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>28</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math **Options:**

- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of plane passing through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, <math close="|" ### Q29. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the foot of the perpendicular drawn from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> to the line passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and parallel the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math>

<p>We know that,</p><p>Direction ratio of line parallel the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi ### Q30. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the plane containing the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> pass through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then the distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> from the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p>Equation of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> </p><p>Now we know that equation of plane passing through the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now given it is passing through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>,</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>12</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> when passing through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>\</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>And when passing through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> we get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>So, from equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math> we get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p>So, point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>And distance of point<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>  from the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>18</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>
