### Q31. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from the plane containing the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of plane passing through the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Required distance is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math> units</p>

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> be the foot of perpendicular drawn from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is a point on the plane such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8736;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>, then the area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> be the foot of perpendicular drawn from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is a point on the plane such that <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8736;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>, then the area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi></math> is equal to</p><p>Now plotting the diagram of given value we get,</p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/634ca675-ce74-473f-baa7-ba63ae1fec71-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 180px;" /></p><p>Now given plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math></p><p>Length of perpendicular from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>to</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Q</mi></math> will be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>Now in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#9651;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>cot</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math></p><p>So,  Area of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the foot of the perpendicular from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>, is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>, then the distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> from the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>, measured parallel to a line with direction ratios <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math>, is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>26</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>26</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Plotting the data of the given value we have,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/13e53736-24de-49d2-9fd2-e337b1bda8a4-image.png" style="width: 189px; height: 120px;" /></p><p>Now let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> be foot of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8869;</mo></math>coordinates, so given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now direction ratio of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> will be  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="&#62;" open="&#60;"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced close="&#62;" open="&#60;"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced close="&#62;" open="&#60;"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> </p><p>Now comparing with <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math> we get, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>So equation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi></math> which is passing through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> will be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p>So point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, but given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi></math> lies on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>, so on satisfying the point we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>C</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now finding the distance between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>C</mi></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>A</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>26</mn></msqrt></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is parallel to two lines whose direction ratios are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math>, and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and it contains the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> intersect the co-ordinate axes at the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi></math> making the intercepts <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi></math> is the volume of the tetrahedron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> is the origin and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math>, then the ordered pair <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>48</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>24</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>48</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>24</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>24</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given a plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is parallel to two lines whose direction ratios are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math>, and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math>,</p><p>So normal of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Now equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> which passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now, coordinates of plane making intercepts at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>Y</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>Z</mi></math> axis will be  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi>C</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></math></p><p>Now finding volume of tetrahedron <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>C</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>48</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>24</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>24</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p></blockquote>

### Q35. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 1)

- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi></math>

<details>

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Let the point on the circle be <math <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, </p><p><img xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mn>14</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>h</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow><mn>14</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Now again from equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>14</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>18</mn></mrow><mn>14</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Now eliminating <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math> from equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>h</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Again from equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>sin</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mn>14</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Now from equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mn>4</mn></mfenced></math> we get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>w</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>h</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi></math></p></blockquote>

### Q36. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> makes an angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> with the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, then the direction cosines of the line are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The vector along the line of intersection will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>a</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>b</mi></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mi>a</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>b</mi></mtd><mtd><mi>c</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>c</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>c</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>i.e. direction ratios of line are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Direction ratio of normal to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Since the line makes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> with the plane, so it will make <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> with its normal.</p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msqrt><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mi>a</mi></math></p><p>So, direction ratios of the line can be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math>  direction cosines can be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#177;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes through the intersection of two mutually perpendicular planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>3</mn></math> and intercepts a unit length on positive <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-axis, then the intercept made by the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> on the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>11</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>11</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> passes through the intersection of two mutually perpendicular planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Since planes are mutually perpendicular</p><p>Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>But <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Now plane passing through these planes will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So intercept on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi></math>-axis <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 2)

The obtuse angle between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> and the vector <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> is

- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<blockquote><p>The normal to the plane determined by the vectors <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> will be vector along the line of intersection will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover accent="true"><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mover open="|"><mover><mi>b</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

### Q39. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> is perpendicular to the two planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>, and passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. If the distance of the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>, then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>21</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>33</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>21</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Given, plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>,</p><p>Whose normal vector is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8801;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Second plane, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math>,</p><p>Whose normal vector is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8801;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now let plane perpendicular to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> will have normal vector <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p>Where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math> through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#175;</mo></mover><mn>3</mn></msub></math> as normal vector will be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>E</mi></math></p><p>Now distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>Now given, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>21</mn></msqrt><mo>&#8658;</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>21</mn></math></p></blockquote>

### Q40. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> be the mirror image of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> with respect to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn></math>. Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math> be a plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> and contains the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi>R</mi></math>. Then, which of the following points lies on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that by image formula, image of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now equation of plane  will be given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd><mtd><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>T</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> </p><p>Now from options, only point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> lies on it.</p>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#215;</mo><mover><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></math> be the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>D</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mi>s</mi></math> of the line of intersection</p><p>Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>16</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mn>15</mn></mfrac><mo>;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>13</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mn>15</mn></mfrac><mo>;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mn>15</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>28</mn></math></p>

### Q42. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math> be the distance between the foot of perpendiculars of the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. Then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math> is equal to ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **26**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>so points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> lie on same side of the given plane.</p><p>Perpendicular distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> from the plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Perpendicular distance of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> from the plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> is parallel to given plane. So, distance between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo></math> distance between their foot of perpendiculars.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mo>-</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>26</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mover><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p>

</details>

### Q43. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the mirror image of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> in the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>c</mi></math> is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>54</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>42</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, equation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now using the formula of image</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>b</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>We get,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>28</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mn>26</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>28</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>58</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>80</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>21</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>116</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>80</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>42</mn><mn>13</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q44. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Let the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi></math> pass through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and is perpendicular to the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn></math></p><p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></math> are integers <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mi>c</mi><mi>d</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>a</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>b</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mi>c</mi></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>d</mi></math> is equal to</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>24</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>22</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that D.R of normal of plane will be given by cross product of given two planes, as it is perpendicular to both given planes, so</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>18</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>So, equation of plane is, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi></math></p><p>And it passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>36</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So equation of plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>18</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>18</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>40</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>22</mn></math> </p>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The acute angle between the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are the planes passing through the intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, respectively, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>12</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of plane passing through the intersection of two planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> can be written as <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and if it is passing through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> then</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>39</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>16</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo></math> Equation of plane through intersection of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>and the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p>Similarly <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo></math> Equation of plane through intersection of</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>29</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Solving we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Angle between planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math> </p>

### Q46. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If two distinct point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi></math> lie on the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>18</mn></msqrt></math> where the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>, then the area of the triangle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi></math> is equal to

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>38</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>38</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>38</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>152</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><msqrt><mn>38</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9b105cdd-743a-4145-b112-baf33870d07c-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 156px;" /></p><p>Given equations of plane are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now a vector perpendicular to both the planes is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>For xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> to the line</p><p><math <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>or <math ### Q48. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is rotated about its line of intersection with the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> by an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>, then the plane after the rotation passes through the point

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given equation of plane, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>We know that equation of family of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8869;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8756;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So, equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>14</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>It passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> be rotated through a right angle about its line of intersection with the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math>. If the mirror image of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> in the rotated plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>, then

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let the equation of the rotated plane be </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>This plane is perpendicular to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn></math></p><p>So the equation of rotated plane will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Also given that the mirror image of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> in rotated plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Equation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>Let coordinate of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So midpoint of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> </p><p>This point will lie on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mi>k</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Therefore <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>8</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>c</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>
