### Q50. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be the plane passing through the intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>, and the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Let the position vectors of the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> respectively. Then the points

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>Y</mi></math> are on the same side of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>X</mi></math> are on the opposite sides of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> are on the opposite sides of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>Y</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>Y</mi></math> are on the same side of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> are on the opposite sides of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know that family of planes equation is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> lies on this plane</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> So the equation of required plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, So <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> are on the opposite sides of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

<p>Let the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">R</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>&#8712;</mo><mi mathvariant="bold-italic">R</mi></math>, intersect at the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math>. If a plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> and is parallel to the lines <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>, then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi></math> is equal to ______.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **5**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math> passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>L</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi></math> passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p>Both the lines intersect at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Normal to the plane will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="]" open="["><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Since plane is parallel to both the given lines, the equation of the plane will be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>On comparing we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q52. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let the points on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be equidistant from the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>. Then the acute angle between the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>6</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>12</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Plotting the diagram of the given information we get,</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0b248344-a308-4f21-90f6-e1dfb8a30917-image.png" style="width: 350px; height: 167px;" /></p><p>Normal vector of plane will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mover><mrow><mi>A</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mrow><mi>O</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now equation of plane is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mi>y</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Assuming other plane to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>P</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup><mo>&#8801;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now the angle between them is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#183;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfenced><mfenced close="|" open="|"><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mn>14</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>14</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>cos</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q53. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let the acute angle bisector of the two planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> be the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>. Then which of the following points lies on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>?</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Angle bisectors of the two given planes are</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>&#177;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><msqrt><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>36</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><mn>13</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>23</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>32</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>&#160;Let&#160;</mtext><mi>&#952;</mi><mtext>&#160;be the angle between the planes&#160;</mtext><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mtext>&#160;and&#160;</mtext><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></msqrt><mo>&#183;</mo><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>16</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>42</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>tan</mi><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>41</mn></msqrt><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math></p><p>Then acute angle bisector is plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>13</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>23</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>32</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math> lies on the the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>)</mo></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>26</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given equation of the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Here, Point on the line is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and the vector in the direction of line is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> then</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9506a6a1-4099-4bd9-ba64-14e969ea4b6d-image.png" style="width: 422px; height: 237px;" /></p><p>from the above diagram <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><msqrt><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mfrac><mn>14</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>Q</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mi>P</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>100</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>16</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>36</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>224</mn><mn>9</mn></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>24</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>6</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> from the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>42</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>34</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>34</mn></msqrt><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>Let, line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is the line of intersection of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> &amp; <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>The direction vector of the line is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mover><mi>n</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>DR&#39;s of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and origin is a common point on both the planes</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Equation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>y</mi><mn>0</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p> </p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2b6afba7-d424-4452-9c97-21e4a5fa3a27-image.png" style="width: 150px; height: 186px;" /></p><p>Now, DR&#39;s of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math ### Q56. JEE Main 2021 (31 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let the equation of the plane, that passes through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and contains the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> then <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi></math> is equal to :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>23</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>23</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>23</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given plane equations are </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Family of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
Passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>20</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>24</mn><mn>36</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Required equation of plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Given equation is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>By comparing above equations</p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>23</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi></math> be the mirror image of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math> with respect to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo></math> be a point of this plane. Then the square of the length of the line segment <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi><mi>R</mi></math> is

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **72**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>be</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>an</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>image</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Q</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo></math>then<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>S</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and</p><p>point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo></math> lies on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>put the points of R in equation of plane anf find the value
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo stretchy="false">&#8658;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">&#8658;</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo stretchy="false">&#8658;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#38;</mi><mi>S</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>using distance formula find</p><p>distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>S</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>36</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>36</mn></msqrt><mo stretchy="false">&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>S</mi><mi>R</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>72</mn></math></p>

</details>

The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and parallel to the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math>-axis, is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>The equation of the plane passing through the line of intersection of the planes:<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math>
Parallel to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo></math>axis</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo stretchy="false">&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#8901;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>j</mi><mo stretchy="false">^</mo></mover><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>3</mn><mover accent="true"><mi>k</mi><mo stretchy="false">^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p>

### Q59. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Equation of a plane at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>21</mn></mfrac></msqrt></math> units from the origin, which contains the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Required equation of plane</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Given that its distance from origin is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msqrt><mn>21</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p>Thus,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><msqrt><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msqrt><mn>21</mn></msqrt></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>21</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>14</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>336</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>168</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>21</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>28</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>16</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>308</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>184</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>308</mn><msup><mi>&#955;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>154</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>30</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mn>154</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mn>15</mn><mn>154</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>, required plane is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be the plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and the line of intersection of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mover accent="true"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></math>. Then which of the following points does NOT lie on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math>?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>16</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>26</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>Now put points given in option and then we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> as required answer.</p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> contains the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math>, and is perpendicular to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. Then which of the following points lies on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>?</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>A plane which containing the line</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></math> is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>This plane is perpendicular to the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> then</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>Equation of plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>9</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>14</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>14</mn></math></p><p>Clearly, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> lies on the plane.</p>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2021 (27 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let the plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to each of the planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo></math> Then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi></math> is equal to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The plane is normal to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So, the normal of required plane is  <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="|" close="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>The required plane is passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>So, the equation of plane is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q63. JEE Main 2021 (22 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> be the line of intersection of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></math> If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is the foot of perpendicular on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> from the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo></math> then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>35</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is equal to:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>101</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>119</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>143</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>134</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>119</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The given planes are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>We can convert these planes into cartesian form by taking <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>x</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Thus, we have <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p>So, let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8801;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/21b03e1e-b04d-4945-bb51-d7082304e804-image.png" style="width: 243px; height: 220px;" /></p><p>Let, the line of Intersection <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> of planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> cuts <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></math> plane in point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> and it is given that the foot of perpendicular <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> from a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> and this point is also on the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></math> plane.</p><p>From the diagram it is clear that, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mi>Q</mi></math> is a line in the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></math> plane and hence at every point on it the <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>z</mi></math>-coordinate is zero. </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>z</mi></math>-coordinate of point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>,</mo></math> is zero and it is on both the planes, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>And, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>On solving the above two equation, we get <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>Q</mi><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math></p></blockquote><blockquote><p>A vector parallel to the line of intersection can be obtained by finding a vector which is perpendicular to both the planes.</p><p>And, we know that a vector perpendicular to two vectors can be obtained by finding their cross product, i.e. the vector parallel to the line of intersection is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable columnalign="right"><mtr><mtd><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Now, we know that the equation of a line passing through a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> and parallel to a vector <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>x</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>y</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>b</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>z</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mi>c</mi></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Thus, the equation of the line of intersection of the planes is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced></mrow><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow><mn>5</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>z</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo></math>let<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#38;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Let, coordinates of foot of perpendicular be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mover><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math></p><p>Now, since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mrow><mi>P</mi><mi>F</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> is perpendicular to the line, hence the dot product of their direction ratios is zero.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>25</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>40</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>35</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>41</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>41</mn><mn>105</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math></p><p>Now, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7</mn><mfenced><mfrac><mn>41</mn><mn>105</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>51</mn><mn>15</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>(</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>51</mn><mn>15</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>119</mn><mo>.</mo></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2021 (20 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> given by the equation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math> Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> be the mirror image of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> with respect to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>.</mo></math> Let a plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> be such that it passes through <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>,</mo></math> and the line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi></math> is perpendicular to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>.</mo></math> Then which of the following points is on the plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>?</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We have,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>L</mi><mo>:</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>k</mi></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>And, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2a7fdc7a-3470-4616-b949-c0c65094ec53-image.png" style="width: 227px; height: 250px;" /></p><p>Direction ratios of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>M</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>,</p><p>Then, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>M</mi><mo>&#8869;</mo><mi>L</mi></math>, therefore</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi><mo>&#8801;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>c</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Also, mid-point of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mi>Q</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>M</mi></math>,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is</p><p><math <p>Equation of plane is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced close="|" open="|"><mtable><mtr><mtd><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mn>6</mn></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mfenced><mn>0</mn></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover accent="true"><mi>a</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mover><mi>i</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>&#946;</mi><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mi>&#947;</mi><mover><mi>k</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
