### Q112. JEE Main 2016 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from the plane passing through the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> and perpendicular to the planes <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math> and <math><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Let equation of plane be

<math><mi>a</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>b</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> .......<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math>

Since <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is perpendicular to the given planes, then</p><p>Dot product of the normal vectors of the perpendicular planes is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>.

<math><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>b</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

Solving above equations <math><mi>c</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math><mi>a</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>b</mi></math> equation of plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mfenced></math> becomes <math><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

Distance from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> will be

<math><mi>D</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q113. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The distance of the point <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> from the plane <math><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> measured along the line <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>20</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>10</mn></msqrt><mi> </mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Equation of line parallel to <math><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>z</mi></math> through

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> is <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></math>

If <math><mi>P&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> be point of intersection of line and plane.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4ff4a7bf-a0c5-4393-b9d3-34de31ad134c-image.png" style="width: 280px; height: 169px;" /></p><p><math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> <math><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Coordinate points are <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>&#8658;</mo></math> Required distance <math><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><msqrt><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>10</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p>

</details>

### Q114. JEE Main 2015 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A plane containing the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>)</mo></math> and the line <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></math> also contains the point

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>7</mn><mo>,</mo><mi> </mi><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

The Direction ratio perpendicular to the plane would be cross product of

<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math> and <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0-</mn><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

= <math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mtable><mtr><mtd><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd><mtd><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>1</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>5</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>4</mn></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mn>2</mn></mtd><mtd><mn>0</mn></mtd><mtd><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mtd></mtr></mtable></mrow></mfenced></math>

= <math><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math>

<math><mo>∴</mo></math> Equation of plane would be

<math><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>λ</mi></math>

As plane contain <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>3,2</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mo>-</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>22</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>λ</mi></math>

<math><mo>-</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>λ</mi></math>

<math><mo>-</mo><mn>15</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>11</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>23</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

This passes through <math><mo>(</mo><mn>0,7</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></math>

</details>

### Q115. JEE Main 2015 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the points <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>&#38;</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math>, are equidistant from the plane, <math><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> then <math><mi>&#955;</mi></math> satisfies the equation:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>x</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>21</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>a</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math> are equidistant from <math><mn>3</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> then

<math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>13</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><msup><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mn>12</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>20</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>12</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi>&#160;</mi><mfenced close="|" open="|" separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>

<math><mn>25</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>30</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>9</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#160;</mi></math>

<math><mn>9</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>30</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>21</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math><mn>3</mn><msup><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>-</mo><mn>10</mn><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

### Q116. JEE Main 2015 (04 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The equation of the plane containing the line of intersection of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>,</mo></math> and parallel to the plane, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Any plane containing the line of intersection of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math> will be of the form

<math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>x</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

It is parallel to plane <math> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#955;</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow><mn>6</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8800;</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>&#955;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math>

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mi>&#955;</mi> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>&#160;&#160;&#160;&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mi>&#955;</mi> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo></math> Required plane is

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>11</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>4</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>5</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>7</mn> <mi>x</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>2</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>2</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>9</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mo>&#8658;&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</mo> <mi>x</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mi>y</mi> <mo>+</mo> <mn>6</mn> <mi>z</mi> <mo>-</mo> <mn>7</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q117. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

If the distance between planes, $4 x-2 y-4 z+1=$ 0 and $4 x-2 y-4 z+d=0$ is 7 , then $d$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 41 or $-42$
- **(B)** 42 or $-43$
- **(C)** $-41$ or 43
- **(D)** $-42$ or 44

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $-41$ or 43**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given planes are
$$
4 x-2 y-4 z+1=0
$$
and $4 x-2 y-4 z+d=0$
They are parallel.
Distance between them is $\pm 7$
$=\frac{d-1}{\sqrt{16+4+16}}$

$$
\begin{aligned}
&\Rightarrow \frac{d-1}{6}=\pm 7 \\
&\Rightarrow d=42+1 \\
&\text { or }-42+1 \\
&\text { i.e. } d=-41 \text { or } 43 .
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

---

### Q118. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A symmetrical form of the line of intersection of the planes $x=a y+b$ and $z=c y+d$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{x-b}{a}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-d}{c}$
- **(B)** $\frac{x-b-a}{a}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-d-c}{c}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{a}}{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-0}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-\mathrm{c}}{\mathrm{d}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{b}-\mathrm{a}}{\mathrm{b}}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-1}{0}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-\mathrm{d}-\mathrm{c}}{\mathrm{d}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{x-b-a}{a}=\frac{y-1}{1}=\frac{z-d-c}{c}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given two planes:
$$
x-a y-b=0 \text { and } c y-z+d=0
$$
Let, $l, m, n$ be the direction ratio of the required line. Since the required line is perpendicular to normal of both the plane, therefore $l-a m=0$ and $c m-n=0$ $\Rightarrow l-a m+0 . n=0$ and $0 . l+c m-n=0$
$$
\therefore \quad \frac{l}{a-0}=\frac{m}{0+1}=\frac{n}{c-0}
$$
Hence, d.R of the required line are $a, 1$, $c$.
Hence, options (c) and (d) are rejected. Now, the point $(a+b, 1, c+d)$ satisfy the equation of the two given planes.
$\therefore$ Option (b) is correct.

</details>

---

### Q119. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The plane containing the line $\frac{\mathrm{x}-1}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}-2}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{z}-3}{3}$ and parallel to the line $\frac{\mathrm{x}}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{y}}{1}=\frac{\mathrm{z}}{4}$ passes through the point:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $(1,-2,5)$
- **(B)** $(1,0,5)$
- **(C)** $(0,3,-5)$
- **(D)** $(-1,-3,0)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $(1,0,5)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of the plane containing the line $\frac{x-1}{1}=\frac{y-2}{2}=\frac{z-3}{3}$ is
$$
a(x-1)+b(y-2)+c(z-3)=0
$$


where $a .1+b .2+c .3=0$
i.e., $\quad a+2 b+3 c=0$
(ii)
Since the plane (i) parallel to the line
$$
\begin{array}{ll} 
& \frac{x}{1}=\frac{y}{1}=\frac{z}{4} \\
\therefore \quad & a .1+b .1+c .4=0 \\
\text { i.e., } \quad & \mathrm{a}+\mathrm{b}+4 \mathrm{c}=0
\end{array}
$$
(iii)
From (ii) and (iii),
$$
\begin{gathered}
\frac{a}{8-3}=\frac{b}{3-4}=\frac{c}{1-2}=k \quad \text { (let) } \\
\therefore \quad a=5 k, b=-k, c=-k
\end{gathered}
$$
On putting the value of $a, b$ and $c$ in equation (i),
$$
\begin{aligned}
&5(x-1)-(y-2)-(z-3)=0 \\
&\Rightarrow 5 x-y-z=0
\end{aligned}
$$
when $x=1, y=0$ and $z=5$; then
L.H.S. of equation (iv) $=5 x-y-2$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&=5 \times 1-0-5 \\
&=0 \\
&=\text { R.H.S. of }
\end{aligned}
$$
equation (iv)
Hence coordinates of the point $(1,0,5)$ satisfy the equation plane represented by equations (iv),
Therefore the plane passes through the point $(1,0,5)$

</details>

### Q122. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The equation of a plane through the line of intersection of the planes $x+2 y=3, y-2 \mathrm{z}+1=0$, and perpendicular to the first plane is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 x-y-10 z=9$
- **(B)** $2 x-y+7 z=11$
- **(C)** $2 x-y+10 z=11$
- **(D)** $2 x-y-9 z=10$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $2 x-y+10 z=11$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of a plane through the line of intersection of the planes $x+2 y=3, y-2 z+1=0$ is $(x+2 y-3)+\lambda(y-2 z+1)=0$
$$
\Rightarrow x+(2+\lambda) y-2 \lambda(z)-3+\lambda=0
$$
Now, plane (i) is $\perp$ to $x+2 y=3$
$\therefore \quad$ Their dot product is zero
i.e. $1+2(2+\lambda)=0 \Rightarrow \lambda=-\frac{5}{2}$
Thus, required plane is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& x+\left(2-\frac{5}{2}\right) y-2 \times \frac{-5}{2}(z)-3-\frac{5}{2}=0 \\
\Rightarrow & x-\frac{y}{2}+5 z-\frac{11}{2}=0 \\
\Rightarrow & 2 x-y+10 z-11=0
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q123. JEE Main 2013 (23 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If the projections of a line segment on the $x, y$ and $z$-axes in 3-dimensional space are 2, 3 and 6 respectively, then the length of the line segment is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** 12
- **(B)** 7
- **(C)** 9
- **(D)** 6

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 7**

**Detailed Solution:**

Length of the line segment
$$
=\sqrt{(2)^2+(3)^2+(6)^2}=7
$$

</details>

### Q124. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $\mathrm{Q}$ be the foot of perpendicular from the origin to the plane $4 x-3 y+z+13=0$ and $R$ be a point $(-1,-6)$ on the plane. Then length $Q R$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\sqrt{14}$
- **(B)** $\sqrt{\frac{19}{2}}$
- **(C)** $3 \sqrt{\frac{7}{2}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{3}{\sqrt{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3 \sqrt{\frac{7}{2}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let $\mathrm{P}$ be the image of $\mathrm{O}$ in the given plane.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/kWvPDyawd72K-gvIc2ilWCk3zbkVv0XXwQBzddVxgu8.original.fullsize.png">

Equation of the plane, $4 x-3 y+z+13=0$ OP is normal to the plane, therefore direction ratio of OP are proportional to $4,-3,1$
Since OP passes through $(0,0,0)$ and has direction ratio proportional to $4,-3,1$. Therefore equation of OP is
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{x-0}{4}=\frac{y-0}{-3}=\frac{z-0}{1}=r \text { (let) } \\
\therefore \quad & x=4 r, y=-3 r, z=r
\end{aligned}
$$
Let the coordinate of $\mathrm{P}$ be $(4 r,-3 r, r)$
Since $Q$ be the mid point of OP
$$
\therefore \quad \mathrm{Q}=\left(2 r,-\frac{3}{2} r, \frac{r}{2}\right)
$$
Since Q lies in the given plane
$$
\begin{aligned}
& 4 x-3 y+z+13=0 \\
\therefore & 8 r+\frac{9}{2} r+\frac{r}{2}+13=0 \\
\Rightarrow & r=\frac{-13}{8+\frac{9}{2}+\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{-26}{26}=-1
\end{aligned}
$$
$\begin{aligned} \therefore \quad & \mathrm{Q}=\left(-2, \frac{3}{2},-\frac{1}{2}\right) \\ & \mathrm{QR}=\sqrt{(-1+2)^2+\left(1-\frac{3}{2}\right)^2+\left(-6+\frac{1}{2}\right)^2} \\ & =\sqrt{1+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{121}{4}}=3 \sqrt{\frac{7}{2}}\end{aligned}$

</details>

---

### Q125. JEE Main 2013 (07 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Distance between two parallel planes <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(B)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>9</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(C)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>
- **(D)** <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>5</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> </mrow> </math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Distance between

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math> and

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>y</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>z</mi><mo>=</mo></math><math> <mrow> <mo>&#8722;</mo><mfrac> <mn>5</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mfrac> </mrow> </math> is

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mfenced open="|" close="|" separators="|"><mrow><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><msqrt><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q126. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider the following planes
$$
\begin{aligned}
& P: x+y-2 z+7=0 \\
& Q: x+y+2 z+2=0 \\
& R: 3 x+3 y-6 z-11=0
\end{aligned}
$$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $P$ and $R$ are perpendicular
- **(B)** $Q$ and $R$ are perpendicular
- **(C)** $P$ and $Q$ are parallel
- **(D)** $P$ and $R$ are parallel

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $P$ and $R$ are parallel**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given planes are
$$
\begin{aligned}
& P: x+y-2 z+7=0 \\
& Q: x+y+2 z+2=0
\end{aligned}
$$
and $R: 3 x+3 y-6 z-11=0$
Consider Plane $P$ and $R$.
Here $a_1=1, b_1=1, c_1=-2$ and $a_2=3, b_2=3, c_2=-6$
Since, $\frac{a_1}{a_2}=\frac{b_1}{b_2}=\frac{c_1}{c_2}=\frac{1}{3}$ therefore $P$ and $R$ are parallel.

</details>

### Q127. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

If the three planes $x=5,2 x-5 a y+3 z-2=0$ and $3 b x+y-3 z=0$ contain a common line, then $(a, b)$ is equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\left(\frac{8}{15},-\frac{1}{5}\right)$
- **(B)** $\left(\frac{1}{5},-\frac{8}{15}\right)$
- **(C)** $\left(-\frac{8}{15}, \frac{1}{5}\right)$
- **(D)** $\left(-\frac{1}{5}, \frac{8}{15}\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\left(\frac{1}{5},-\frac{8}{15}\right)$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the direction ratios of the common line be $\ell, m$ and $n$.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore \ell \times 1+\mathrm{m} \times 0+\mathrm{n} \times 0=0 \Rightarrow \ell=0 \\
& 2 \ell-5 m a+3 n=0 \Rightarrow 5 m a-3 n=0 \\
& 3 \ell b+m-3 n=0 \Rightarrow m-3 n=0
\end{aligned}
$$
Subtracting (3) from (1), we get $m(5 a-1)=0$
Now, value of $m$ can not be zero because if $m=0$ then $n=0$
$\Rightarrow \ell=m=n=0$ which is not possible.
Hence, $5 a-1=0 \Rightarrow a=\frac{1}{5}$

</details>

---

### Q128. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The values of a for which the two points $(1, a, 1)$ and $(-3,0, a)$ lie on the opposite sides of the plane $3 x+4 y-12 z+13=0$, satisfy

**Options:**

- **(A)** $0 < a < \frac{1}{3}$
- **(B)** $-1 < a < 0$
- **(C)** $a < -1$ or $a < \frac{1}{3}$
- **(D)** $a=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $a=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given equation of plane is
$$
3 x+4 y-12 z+13=0
$$
$(1, a, 1)$ and $(-3,0, a)$ satisfy the equation of plane.
$\therefore$ We have
$3+4(a)-12+13=0$ and
$3(-3)-12(a)+13=0$
$\Rightarrow 4+4 a=0$ and $4-12 a=0$
$\Rightarrow a=-1$ and $a=\frac{1}{3}$
Since, $(1, a, 1)$ and $(-3,0, a)$ lie on the opposite sides of the plane $\therefore a=0$

</details>

### Q129. JEE Main 2012 (Offline)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An equation of a plane parallel to the plane $x-2 y+2 z-5=0$ and at a unit distance from the origin is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x-2 y+2 z-3=0$
- **(B)** $x-2 y+2 z+1=0$
- **(C)** $x-2 y+2 z-1=0$
- **(D)** $x-2 y+2 z+5=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $x-2 y+2 z-3=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Equation of plane parallel to $x-2 y+2 z-5=0$ is $x-2 y+2 z+k=0 \dots \dots(1)$ 
perpendicular distance from $\mathrm{O}(0,0,0)$ to $(1)$ is $1$
$\frac{|k|}{\sqrt{1+4+4}}=1 \quad \Rightarrow|k|=3 \quad \Rightarrow k=\pm 3 \quad \therefore x-2 y+2 z-3=0$

</details>

$$
A(3,1,6) ; B=(1,3,4)

and d.r's of $A B=(2,-2,2)$
d.r's Of normal to plane $=(1,-1,1)$.
$\mathrm{AB}$ is perpendicular bisector

### Q131. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Let $L$ be the line of intersection of the planes $2 x+3 y+z=1$ and $x+3 y+2 z=2$. If $L$ makes an angles $\alpha$ with the positive $x$-axis, then $\cos \alpha$ equals

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{2}$
- **(C)** $1$
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

If direction cosines of $L$ be $l$, $m, n$, then
$\begin{aligned}
& 2 l+3 m+n=0 \\
& l+3 m+2 n=0
\end{aligned}$
Solving, we get, $\frac{l}{3}=\frac{m}{-3}=\frac{n}{3}$
$\therefore l: m: n=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}:-\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}: \frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \Rightarrow \cos \alpha=\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}} \text {. }$

</details>

### Q133. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The plane $x+2 y-z=4$ cuts the sphere $x^2+y^2+z^2-x+z-2=0$ in a circle of radius

**Options:**

- **(A)** 3
- **(B)** 1
- **(C)** 2
- **(D)** $\sqrt{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 1**

**Detailed Solution:**

Perpendicular distance of centre $\left(\frac{1}{2}, 0,-\frac{1}{2}\right)$ from $x+2 y-2=4$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\left|\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}-4\right|}{\sqrt{6}}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}} \\
& \text { radius }=\sqrt{\frac{5}{2}-\frac{3}{2}}=1 .
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q135. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Distance between two parallel planes $2 x+y+2 z=8$ and $4 x+2 y+4 z+5=0$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3}{2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{5}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{7}{2}$
- **(D)** $\frac{9}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{7}{2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given planes are
$$
2 x+y+2 z-8=0,4 x+2 y+4 z+5=0 \Rightarrow 2 x+y+2 z+5 / 2=0
$$
Distance between planes $=\frac{\left|d_1-d_2\right|}{\sqrt{a^2+b^2+c^2}}=\frac{|-8-5 / 2|}{\sqrt{2^2+1^2+2^2}}=\frac{7}{2}$

</details>

### Q136. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Two system of rectangular axes have the same origin. If a plane cuts them at distances $\mathrm{a}, \mathrm{b}, \mathrm{c}$ and $\mathrm{a}^{\prime}, \mathrm{b}^{\prime}, \mathrm{c}^{\prime}$ from the origin then

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{a^2}+\frac{1}{b^2}+\frac{1}{c^2}-\frac{1}{a^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{b^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{c^{\prime 2}}=0$
- **(B)** $\frac{1}{a^2}+\frac{1}{b^2}+\frac{1}{c^2}+\frac{1}{a^{22}}+\frac{1}{b^{22}}+\frac{1}{c^{22}}=0$
- **(C)** $\frac{1}{\mathrm{a}^2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{~b}^2}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{c}^2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{a}^{\prime 2}}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{~b}^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{\mathrm{c}^{\prime 2}}=0$
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{a^2}-\frac{1}{b^2}-\frac{1}{c^2}+\frac{1}{a^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{b^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{c^{\prime 2}}=0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{1}{a^2}+\frac{1}{b^2}+\frac{1}{c^2}-\frac{1}{a^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{b^{\prime 2}}-\frac{1}{c^{\prime 2}}=0$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Eq. of planes be $\frac{\mathrm{x}}{\mathrm{a}}+\frac{\mathrm{y}}{\mathrm{b}}+\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{c}}=1 \& \frac{\mathrm{x}}{\mathrm{a}_1}+\frac{\mathrm{y}}{\mathrm{b}_1}+\frac{\mathrm{z}}{\mathrm{c}_1}=1(\perp \mathrm{r}$ distance on plane from origin is same. $)$
$\left|\frac{-1}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{\mathrm{a}^2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{~b}^2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{c}^2}}}\right|=\left|\frac{-1}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{\mathrm{a}_1^2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{~b}_1^2}+\frac{1}{\mathrm{c}_1^2}}}\right|$
$\therefore \Sigma \frac{1}{a^2}-\Sigma \frac{1}{a_1^2}=0$

</details>

### Q137. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The shortest distance from the plane $12 x+4 y+3 z=327$ to the sphere $x^2+y^2+z^2+4 x-2 y-6 z=155$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** 39
- **(B)** 26
- **(C)** $11 \frac{4}{13}$
- **(D)** 13

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) 13**

**Detailed Solution:**

Shortest distance $=$ perpendicular distance $=\left|\frac{-2 \times 12+4 \times 1+3 \times 3-327}{\sqrt{144+9+16}}\right|=26$
$\therefore$ Shortest distance
$$=26-\sqrt{4+1+15+9}=26-13=13 \quad[\because 26-r]$$

</details>
